---
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
For $k\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}\xspace$ and $v\in  
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D}}{ 
		\ensuremath{V}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt V}\!D} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
$, the \emph{earliest dispatch time} of commodity $k$ at node $v$ is $r_k+\ifthenelse{\isempty{\tau}}{ 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{o_k,v}}{ 
		\ensuremath{d}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{o_k,v}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt d}o_k,v} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{d\parentheses{o_k,v}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{d\parentheses{o_k,v}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
}{ 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{o_k,v}}{ 
		\ensuremath{d_{\tau}}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{o_k,v}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt d_{\tau}}o_k,v} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{d_{\tau}\parentheses{o_k,v}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{d_{\tau}\parentheses{o_k,v}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
}$ and is denoted by $\underline{t}_{k}({v})$. The \emph{latest dispatch time} of $k$ at $v$, $\overline{t}_{k}({v})$, is $\ell_k-\ifthenelse{\isempty{\tau}}{ 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{v,d_k}}{ 
		\ensuremath{d}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{v,d_k}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt d}v,d_k} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{d\parentheses{v,d_k}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{d\parentheses{v,d_k}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
}{ 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{v,d_k}}{ 
		\ensuremath{d_{\tau}}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{v,d_k}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt d_{\tau}}v,d_k} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{d_{\tau}\parentheses{v,d_k}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{d_{\tau}\parentheses{v,d_k}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
}$.
	
	We extend the notation to arcs $a=vv'$ by setting $\underline{t}_{k}({a}) = \underline{t}_{k}({v})$ and $\overline{t}_{k}({a})=\overline{t}_{k}({v'}) - \tau(a)$.
	Thus, the interval in which a commodity $k$ can be dispatched on an arc $a$ is specified by $I_k(a) = [\underline{t}_{k}({a}),\overline{t}_{k}({a})]$.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- observation-1: body_md -->
\label{intervals-implementable-dt}
	Let $S=(\ensuremath{\mathcal{P}}\xspace,\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\xspace)$ be an implementable solution with dispatch times $t\colon\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\xspace\to\ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}\xspace$. Then, for $c=(a,\kappa)\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\xspace$ and $k\in\kappa$, $t(c)\in I_k(a)$.
<!-- end observation-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
Let $k\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}\xspace$ with $P_k = a_1\ldots a_n$ and $c_i=c(a_i,k)$.
	Then $t(c_1) \geq r_k = \underline{t}_{k}({a_1})$ and, inductively, $t(c_{i+1}) \geq t(c_i) + \tau(a_i) \geq \underline{t}_{k}({a_i}) + \tau(a_i) \geq \underline{t}_{k}({a_{i+1}})$ for $i\in (n)$.
	
	Similarly, $t(c_n) \leq \ell_k - \tau(a_n) = \overline{t}_{k}({a_n})$ and $t(c_i) \leq t(c_{i+1}) - \tau(a_i) \leq \overline{t}_{k}({a_{i+1}}) - \tau(a_i) \leq \overline{t}_{k}({a_i})$ for $i\in(n)$ and the claim follows since for each $c=(a,\kappa)\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\xspace$ and $k\in\kappa$, $a\inP_k$.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
A \emph{time discretisation} $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$ consists of a set $
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { 0,H } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiter0,H\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{0,H\right\}} 
	 }
 
\subseteq \ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_a\subseteq[H]$ of time points for each arc $a\in  
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D}}{ 
		\ensuremath{A}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt A}D} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
$.
	If $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_a = 
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { t_0,\ldots,t_n } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimitert_0,\ldots,t_n\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{t_0,\ldots,t_n\right\}} 
	 }
 
$ with $0=t_0< t_1<\ldots<t_n = H$, then we define $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_a^I = 
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { [t_0,t_1),[t_1,t_2),\ldots,[t_{n-1},t_n) } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiter[t_0,t_1),[t_1,t_2),\ldots,[t_{n-1},t_n)\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{[t_0,t_1),[t_1,t_2),\ldots,[t_{n-1},t_n)\right\}} 
	 }
 
$ as the intervals corresponding to the discretisation.
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\fontfamily{cmr}\textup{\textsc{R-SND}}\xspace{} is a relaxation of \fontfamily{cmr}\textup{\textsc{SND}}\xspace.
	For a full discretisation, it is tight.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Let $S=(\ensuremath{\mathcal{P}}\xspace,\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\xspace)$ be a solution and $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$ be a time discretisation.
	If $S$ is implementable, there exist dispatch times $t\colon\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\xspace\to\ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}\xspace$.
	For $c\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\xspace$, we let $h(c)$ be the interval in $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace^I_{a(c)}$ containing $t(c)$.
	
	Let $k\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}\xspace$ with $P_k = a_1\ldots a_n$ and $c_i = c(a_i,k)$ for $i\in(n]$.
	The first property holds since $t(c_i) \in h(c_i)\cap I_k(a_i)$ by \cref{intervals-implementable-dt}.
	Finally, since $t(c_{i+1})\geq t(c_i) + \tau(a_i)$, we get $\min h(c_i) + \tau(a_i) \leq \max h(c_{i+1})$ as well.
	Thus, $S$ is $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-representable.
	
	If $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$ is the full discretisation and $S$ is $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-representable, then we get single element dispatch intervals $h(c)$ for $c=(a,\kappa)\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\xspace$ and we set $t(c)$ to the single element of $h(c)$.
	Since $h(c,k) \coloneqq h(c) \cap I_k(a) \neq \varnothing$ for all $k\in\kappa$, $t(c)$ is actually equal to the element in $h(c,k)$ as well. 
	
	Again, let $k\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}\xspace$ with $P_k = a_1\ldots a_n$ and $c_i = c(a_i,k)$ for $i\in(n]$.
	Then $t(c_1) = \min h(c_1,k) \geq \underline{t}_{k}({a_1}) = \underline{t}_{k}({o_k}) = r_k$, $t(c_{i+1}) = \max h(c_{i+1}) \geq \min h(c_{i}) + \tau(a_i) = t(c_i) + \tau(a_i)$, and $t(c_n) + \tau(a_n) = \max h(c_n,k) + \tau(a_n) \leq \overline{t}_{k}({a_n}) + \tau(a_n) = \overline{t}_{k}({d_k}) = \ell_k$.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
Given a solution $S=(\ensuremath{\mathcal{P}}\xspace,\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\xspace)$, its \emph{dispatch-node graph} $D_S$ is given by

	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{({v},{k}) : k\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}\xspace,\, v\in P_k\right\}}

, \\

 &=

.

	For $b=({v},{k})({v'},{k'})\in

$, we write $a(b)=vv'$ for its underlying arc in $D$, and set $\tau(b) = \tau(a(b))$ and $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_b = \ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{a(b)}$.
	For $u=({v},{k})\in

$, we set $\underline{t}_{}({u}) = \underline{t}_{k}({v})$ and $\overline{t}_{}({u})=\overline{t}_{k}({v})$.

<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
For a walk $W=u_0\ldots u_n$ in $D_S$ and $i\in [n]$, we define the real arrival time $t_{W}(i) = \underline{t}_{}({u_0}) + \tau(W u_i)$ and call $W$ a too-long path if $t_{W}(n) > \overline{t}_{}({u_n})$.
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
This definition differs slightly from that of~\textcite[][Definition 2]{shu_new_2026}; they require that a too-long path starts at a commodity's origin, i.e., $u = (o_k, k)$ for some $k \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}\xspace$. 
    In most respects, both definitions yield essentially the same results, but the subsequent discussion is easier to follow with our definition, since it allows us to disregard various edge cases.
	We note that to stay consistent with their terminology, we say too-long path even though walks are also permitted.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{characterisation:implementable}
	A solution $S$ is implementable if and only if the corresponding dispatch-node graph $D_S$ contains no too-long path.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Let $S=(\ensuremath{\mathcal{P}}\xspace,\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\xspace)$ be implementable with dispatch times $t\colon\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\xspace\to\ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}\xspace$.
	Moreover, let $W=u_0\ldots u_n$ be a walk in $D_S$ with $u_i=({v_i},{k_i})$, for $i\in[n]$ and $a_i=v_{i-1}v_i$, and $c_i = c(a_i,k_i)$ for $i\in(n]$.
	
	Since, for $i\in(n)$, $c_{i}$ and $c_{i+1}$ are consolidations on consecutive arcs of $P_{k_{i}}$, we get $t(c_{i+1}) \geq t(c_{i}) + \tau(a_i)$ and, thus, $t(c_n) + \tau(a_n) \geq t(c_1) + \tau(W)$.
	By \cref{intervals-implementable-dt}, $t({c_1}) \geq \underline{t}_{k_0}({a_1}) =  \underline{t}_{k_0}({v_0}) = \underline{t}_{}({u_0})$ and $t(c_n)\leq \overline{t}_{k_{n}}({a_n}) = \overline{t}_{k_n}({v_n}) - \tau(a_n) = \overline{t}_{}({u_n}) - \tau(a_n)$, so $W$ is not too-long.
	
	Conversely, assume that $D_S$ has no too-long path.
	Then, in particular, $D_S$ is acyclic.
	By \cref{ndg-in-out}, the nodes without incoming arcs are exactly the nodes $({o_k},{k})$ for $k\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}\xspace$, for which we define $t(({o_k},{k})) = r_k$.
	For the remaining nodes $u$, we set $t(u) = \max

	\ifthenelse{\isempty{u}}{ 
		\ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}}\xspace 
	}{

	{u}

        } 
    }{

} } {

	\ifthenelse{\isempty{u}}{ 
		\ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}}\xspace

}{

		\ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}_{}}\xspace

        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{u}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{

}{

	{u}

}\right\}}

$, which is well-defined as $D_S$ is acyclic.
	Finally, for $c=(vv',\kappa)\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\xspace$, we define $t(c) = \max
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { t(({v},{k})) : k\in\kappa } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimitert(({v},{k})) : k\in\kappa\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{t(({v},{k})) : k\in\kappa\right\}} 
	 }
 
$.
	
	We first note that, by \cref{ndg-in-out}, $\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}{ 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{({v},{k})}}{ 
		\ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{({v},{k})}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt N^{\text{in}}}({v},{k})} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}\parentheses{({v},{k})}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}\parentheses{({v},{k})}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
}{ 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{({v},{k})}}{ 
		\ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}_{}}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{({v},{k})}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt N^{\text{in}}_{}}({v},{k})} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}_{}\parentheses{({v},{k})}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}_{}\parentheses{({v},{k})}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
} = 
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { ({v'},{k'}) : k'\in \kappa } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiter({v'},{k'}) : k'\in \kappa\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{({v'},{k'}) : k'\in \kappa\right\}} 
	 }
 
$, where $v'$ is the predecessor of $v$ in $P_k$ and $c=c(v'v,k) = (v'v,\kappa)$.
	In particular, $t(u) = t(c) + \tau(v'v)$.
	
	We now simply verify that $t$ satisfies the desired properties.
	Let $k\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}\xspace$ with $P_k = a_1\ldots a_n$, $c_i = c(a_i,k) = (a_i,\kappa_i)$, and $a_i=v_{i-1}v_i$ for $i\in(n]$.
	Then $t(c_1) \geq t(({o_k},{k})) = r_k$ and, for $i\in(n)$, $t(c_{i+1}) \geq t(({v_i},{k})) = t(c_i) + \tau(a_i)$.
	
	Finally, we show that for each $u\in D_S$ there exists a $u'u$-path $W$ such that $t(u)\leq \underline{t}_{}({u'}) + \tau(W)$.
	This implies the final property, since applied to $u=({v_n},{k})=({d_k},{k})$ we obtain $t(c_n) + \tau(a_n) = t(u) \leq \underline{t}_{}({u'}) + \tau(W) \leq \overline{t}_{}({u}) = \ell_k$.
	
	To see that such paths exist, we use the topological ordering we get from acyclicity.
	For the induction start, we know that $u=({o_k},{k})$ for some $k\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}\xspace$, for which $t(u) = r_k = \underline{t}_{}({u})$, so we can use the trivial path $W=u$.
	In the induction step, we get that $t(u) = t(u'')+ \tau(u''u)$ for some $u''\in\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}{ 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{u}}{ 
		\ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{u}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt N^{\text{in}}}u} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}\parentheses{u}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}\parentheses{u}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
}{ 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{u}}{ 
		\ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}_{}}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{u}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{

        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}_{}\parentheses{u}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
}$.
	By the induction hypothesis, we get a $u'u''$-path $W'$ satisfying $t(u'') \leq \underline{t}_{}({u'}) + \tau(W')$.
	We extend this path by the arc $u''u$ to get a $u'u$-path~$W$ satisfying $t(u) = t(u'')+\tau(u''u) \leq \underline{t}_{}({u'}) + \tau(W)$ as required.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
Let $S$ be a solution, $W$ be a walk in $D_S$, and $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$ be a time discretisation.
	We say $W$ is \emph{eliminated} by $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$ if there exists no $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-representable solution $S'$ whose dispatch-node graph $D_{S'}$ contains $W$.
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- observation-3: body_md -->
\label{obs:elimination_of_extensions}
	If a walk $W$ is eliminated by $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$, then all extensions $W'$ of $W$ are also eliminated by $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$.
<!-- end observation-3 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
If $W'$ is not eliminated, there exists a solution $S'$ that is $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-representable such that $W'$ appears in $D_{S'}$.
	But then $W$ also appears in $D_{S'}$.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
A too-long path is \emph{minimal} if none of its proper sub-walks are too-long.
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
A solution $S$ is implementable if and only if the corresponding dispatch-node graph $D_S$ contains no minimal too-long path.
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
\label{def:relaxed_arrival_times}
	Let $W=u_0b_1u_1\ldots b_nu_n$ be a walk in $D_S$ and $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$ be a time discretisation.
	We define the \emph{relaxed arrival time $\pi_{W}(i)$} at the $i$th node of $W$ by
	\begin{displaymath}
		\pi_{W}(i) = 
		\begin{cases}
			\underline{t}_{}({u_0}) & \text{if $i=0$,}\\
			\lfloor\pi_{W}(i-1)\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{b_{i}}} +\tau(b_{i}) & \text{otherwise}
		\end{cases}
	\end{displaymath}
	where $\lfloort\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{a}} = \max
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { t'\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_a : t'\leq t } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimitert'\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_a : t'\leq t\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{t'\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_a : t'\leq t\right\}} 
	 }
 
$.
	If $\pi_{W}(n) > \overline{t}_{}({u_n})$, we say \(W\) is $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-\emph{relaxed too-long}.
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
\label{remark:relaxed_arrival_time_definition}
	In the short paper~\cite{Wullner2026-xl} we say a too-long path is eliminated if and only if it fulfils this criterion, which is not consistent with the terminology in this paper.
	The idea behind this criterion was already used by~\textcite[][Theorem 3]{helber_arc-based_2026} to check if too-long paths are already eliminated.
	A stronger variant of the criterion is also introduced in the newer version of the preprint~\textcite[][Lemma~2]{shu_new_2026}.
	We discuss how our contributions can be adapted to their criterion in~\cref{sec:variants}, but do not use it for our exposition since it increases notational burden and edge cases without yielding more interesting results.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{characterisation:representable}
	Let $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$ be a time discretisation.
	A solution $S$ is $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-representable if and only if $D_S$ contains no $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long path.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Let $S=(\ensuremath{\mathcal{P}}\xspace,\ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\xspace)$ be $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-representable with dispatch intervals $h\colon \ensuremath{\mathcal{C}}\xspace\to2^\ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}\xspace$.
	Moreover, let $W=u_0\ldots u_n$ be a walk in $D_S$ with $u_i = ({v_i},{k_i})$ for $i\in[n]$ and $a_i=v_{i-1}v_i$, $c_i=c(a_i,k_i)=c(a_i,k_{i-1})$ for $i\in(n]$.
	
	We show that $\pi_{W}(i-1)\leq \max h(c_{i})$, for all $i\in(n]$.
	For $i=1$, since $h(c_1)\cap I_{k_0}(a_1)\neq\varnothing$, $\max h(c_1) \geq \underline{t}_{k_0}({a_1}) = \underline{t}_{}({u_0}) = \pi_{W}(0)$.
	For $i\in(n)$, using the induction hypothesis and the fact that $S$ is $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-representable, $\pi_{W}(i) = \lfloor\pi_{W}(i-1)\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{a_i}}  + \tau(a_{i})\leq \lfloor\max h(c_{i})\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{a_i}} + \tau(a_{i}) = \min h(c_{i}) + \tau(a_i) \leq \max h(c_{i+1})$.
	
	Since $h(c_n)\cap I_{k_n}(a_n)\neq\varnothing$, $\min h(c_n)\leq \overline{t}_{k_n}({a_n}) = \overline{t}_{}({u_n})-\tau(a_n)$ and, consequently, $\pi_{W}(n) = \lfloor\pi_{W}(n-1)\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{a_n}} + \tau(a_n) \leq \lfloor\max h(c_{n})\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{a_n}} + \tau(a_n) = \min h(c_n) + \tau(a_n) \leq \overline{t}_{}({u_n})$.
	Thus, $W$ is not relaxed too-long.
	
	For the converse, we assume that $D_S$ contains no relaxed too-long path.
	This lets us define a dispatch time at every node $u$ in $D_S$ by setting
	\begin{displaymath}
		t(u)=\max
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { \pi_{W}(n) : W \text{ is a walk in $D_S$ ending at $u$} } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiter\pi_{W}(n) : W \text{ is a walk in $D_S$ ending at $u$}\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{\pi_{W}(n) : W \text{ is a walk in $D_S$ ending at $u$}\right\}} 
	 }
 
.
	\end{displaymath}
	Note that we explicitly allow walks of length 0 here, meaning $t(u) \geq \underline{t}_{}({u})$.
	Also, since no relaxed too-long paths exist, this value is finite and satisfies $t(u) \leq \overline{t}_{}({u})$.
	For $c=(vv',\kappa)$, we let $t(c)=\max
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { t(({v},{k})) : k\in\kappa } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimitert(({v},{k})) : k\in\kappa\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{t(({v},{k})) : k\in\kappa\right\}} 
	 }
 
$ and $h(c)$ be the interval in $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace^I_{vv'}$ containing $t(c)$.
	We use this to show that $S$ is $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-representable.
	
	To this end, let $k\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}\xspace$ with $P_k = a_1\ldots a_n$, $c_i = c(a_i,k)=(a_i,\kappa_i)$, and $a_i=v_{i-1}v_i$ for $i\in(n]$.
	Furthermore, let $u_i=({v_i},{k})$ for $i\in[n]$.
	By definition, for $i\in(n]$, $\max h(c_i) \geq t(c_i) \geq t(u_{i-1}) \geq \underline{t}_{}({u_{i-1}}) = \underline{t}_{k}({a_i})$.
	
	Again, let $i\in (n]$, and let $u'=(v_{i-1},k')$ with $k'\in\kappa_i$ such that $t(c_i) = t(u')$.
	Moreover, let $W$ be a walk of length $m\geq 0$ in $D_S$ ending at $u'$ with $\pi_{W}(m) = t(u') = t(c_i)$.
	By extending $W$ to $u_i$, we get a walk $W'$ ending at $u_i$ and, thus, $t(u_i) \geq \pi_{W'}(m+1) = \lfloort(c_i)\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{a_i}} + \tau(a_i)$.
	Since $W'$ is not relaxed too-long, $\pi_{W'}(m+1) \leq \overline{t}_{}({u_i}) = \overline{t}_{k}({a_i})+\tau(a_i)$.
	Hence, $\min h(c_i) = \lfloort(c_i)\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{a_i}} \leq \overline{t}_{k}({a_i})$ and $h(c_i)\cap I_k(a_i)\neq \varnothing$.


	For the final property, let $i\in(n)$.
	Using the above computations, we see that $\min h(c_i) + \tau(a_i) = \lfloort(c_i)\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{a_i}} + \tau(a_i) \leq t(u_i) \leq t(c_{i+1}) \leq \max h(c_{i+1})$ and the proof is complete.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- observation-4: body_md -->
\label{obs:eliminated_if_rtl}
	A too-long path $W$ is eliminated by a discretisation $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$ if it is $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long.
<!-- end observation-4 -->
<!-- example-1: body_md -->
We demonstrate that the converse of~\cref{obs:eliminated_if_rtl} is not true; a too-long path may be eliminated by \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace\) even if it is not \(\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace\)-relaxed too-long.
	For non-minimal paths this follows directly from~\cref{obs:elimination_of_extensions}, but it is not at all obvious that the same is true for minimal paths.
	Consider the example in \cref{fig:eliminated_but_not_rtl}, especially the minimal too-long path $W$.
	We select a discretisation $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$ that is full (i.e., $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_a = [H]$) except that $5 \notin \ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{v_2v_3}$ and $6 \notin \ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{v_3v_4}$.
	Then $W$ is not relaxed too-long, since $\pi_{W}(0) = 2, \pi_{W}(1) = 3, \pi_{W}(2) = 5, \pi_{W}(3) = 6, \pi_{W}(4) = 7$, and $\pi_{W}(5) = 9 \not> 9 = \ell_{k_1}$.
	For this path to exist in a solution's dispatch-node graph, commodity $k_1$ must traverse $v_0v_1$ together with $k_3$, as well as $v_1v_2$ and $v_4v_5$.
	Taking $v_2v_5$ would prevent it from traversing the required arc $v_4v_5$, so it must take $v_2v_3$.
	This implies that $W'$ also exists in the dispatch-node graph, and this path is relaxed too-long, since $\pi_{W'}(0) = 2, \pi_{W'}(1) = 3, \pi_{W'}(2) = 5, \pi_{W'}(3) = 6 > 5 = \overline{t}_{k_1}({v_3})$.
	Since existence of $W$ implies $W'$ and this path is relaxed too-long, both of them are eliminated.
	
	\begin{figure}[htbp]
		\centering
		\begin{tikzpicture}[>=stealth]
			\node[draw, label={[align=left]above left:{$r_{k_1}=r_{k_2}=0$\\$r_{k_3}=2$}}] (v0) at (0,0) {$v_0$};
			\node[draw] (v1) at (2,0) {$v_1$};
			\node[draw] (v2) at (4,0) {$v_2$};
			\node[draw] (v3) at (6,0) {$v_3$};
			\node[draw] (v4) at (8,0) {$v_4$};
			\node[draw, label={[align=left]above right:{$\ell_{k_1}=9$\\$\ell_{k_2}=12$\\$\ell_{k_3}=12$}}] (v5) at (10,0) {$v_5$};
			
\draw[->, thick] (v0) to node[above]{1} (v1);
			\draw[->, thick] (v1) to node[above]{2} (v2);
			\draw[->, thick] (v2) to node[above]{2} (v3);
			\draw[->, thick] (v3) to node[above]{2} (v4);
			\draw[->, thick] (v4) to node[above]{2} (v5);
			\draw[->] (v0.north) .. controls +(0,2) and +(0,2) ..
			node[above]{2} (v4.north);
			\draw[->] (v2.south) .. controls +(0,-1) and +(0,-1) ..
			node[below]{4} (v5.south);
			
			\node[align=left, anchor=north west, draw, label={[shift=(solbox.north west)]above right:Solution $S$}] (solbox) at (-1.5,-2) {
				$P_{k_1}=P_{k_2}=v_0v_1v_2v_3v_4v_5$\\
				$P_{k_3}=v_0v_1v_2v_5$\\
				$c_1 = (v_0v_1, 
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { k_1,k_3 } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiterk_1,k_3\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{k_1,k_3\right\}} 
	 }
 
)$\\
				$c_2 = (v_1v_2, 
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { k_1,k_2 } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiterk_1,k_2\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{k_1,k_2\right\}} 
	 }
 
)$\\
				$c_3 = (v_4v_5, 
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { k_1,k_2 } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiterk_1,k_2\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{k_1,k_2\right\}} 
	 }
 
)$
			};
			\node[align=left, label={[shift=(tlps.north west)]above right:Too-long paths}, draw, anchor=north west, xshift=5mm] (tlps) at (solbox.north east) {
				$W=(v_0,k_3)(v_1,k_1)(v_2,k_2)(v_3,k_2)(v_4,k_2)(v_5,k_1)$\\
				$W' = (v_0,k_3)(v_1,k_1)(v_2,k_1)(v_3,k_1)$
			};
		\end{tikzpicture}
		\caption{Example of a minimal too-long path that is eliminated by $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$ but not $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long. Arcs are labelled with travel time. Commodities' origins and destinations are labelled with their release time and deadline.
		All unlisted consolidations are singletons.
		}
		\label{fig:eliminated_but_not_rtl}
	\end{figure}
<!-- end example-1 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
In the short paper \cite{Wullner2026-xl}, we show that under this objective function, the problem of making an arbitrary set of too-long paths also relaxed too-long is NP-hard. 
	We note that it is possible to construct instances of \fontfamily{cmr}\textup{\textsc{SND}}\xspace such that the hardness result transfers to ORP as stated here, but omit the construction, which provides little additional insight.
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
Let $u\in 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_S}}{ 
		\ensuremath{V}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_S}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt V}\!D_S} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_S}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_S}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
$, $t\in \ensuremath{\mathbb{N}}\xspace$, and $w=(u,t)$.
	We define the \emph{slack consumption} and \emph{lateness} at $w$ by 
	\begin{equation*}
		\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w) = t - \underline{t}_{}({u}), \qquad \operatorname{\Delta_L}(w) = t - \overline{t}_{}({u}).
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- observation-5: body_md -->
\label{used-buffer-lateness-minimal-tlp}
	If $W=u_0\ldots u_n$ is a minimal too-long path and $w_i=(u_i,t_{W}(i))$, then the following properties hold:
	\begin{itemize}
		\item $\operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n) > 0$ and $\operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_i)\leq 0$ for all $i\in[n)$.
		\item $\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w_0) = 0$ and $\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w_i) \geq \operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n) > 0$ for all $i\in(n]$.
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end observation-5 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
For the lateness, $\operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n) > 0$ since $W$ is too-long and, for $i\in[n)$, $\operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_i) \leq 0$ since $W u_i$ is not too-long.
	
	For the slack consumption, $\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w_0) = 0$ since $t_{W}(0) = \underline{t}_{}({u_0})$, and, for $i\in(n]$, $u_iW$ is not too-long, so $\underline{t}_{}({u_i}) + \tau(u_iW) \leq \overline{t}_{}({u_n}) = t_{W}(n) - \operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n) = \underline{t}_{}({u_0}) + \tau(W) -\operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n) = t_{W}(i) + \tau(u_iW) - \operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n)$.
	Thus, $t_{W}(i) \geq \underline{t}_{}({u_i}) +\operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n)$ and $\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w_i) \geq \operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n)$.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{used-buffer-lateness-minimal-tlp-equiv}
	If $W=u_0\ldots u_n$ is a walk in $D_S$ and $w_i=(u_i,t_{W}(i))$, then $W$ is minimal too-long if and only if
	\begin{itemize}
		\item $\operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_i)\leq 0$ for all $i\in[n)$ and
		\item $\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w_i) \geq \operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n) > 0$ for all $i\in(n]$.
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
The forward implication follows from \cref{used-buffer-lateness-minimal-tlp}.
	For the converse, let $W=u_0\ldots u_n$ be a walk satisfying the given properties and $w_i=(u_i,t_{W}(i))$.
	Since $\operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n)>0$, $W$ is a too-long path.
	Let $u_iW u_j$ be a sub-walk of $W$ that is too-long.
	If $j<n$, then $\underline{t}_{}({u_i}) + \tau(u_iW u_j) \leq \underline{t}_{}({u_0}) + \tau(u_0W u_j) = t_{W}(j) = \operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_j) + \overline{t}_{}({u_j}) \leq \overline{t}_{}({u_j})$.
	Thus, $j=n$.
	
	If $i>0$, then $\underline{t}_{}({u_i}) + \tau(u_iW) = t_{W}(i) - \operatorname{\Delta_S}(w_i) + \tau(u_iW) = \underline{t}_{}({u_0}) + \tau(W) - \operatorname{\Delta_S}(w_i) \leq t_{W}(n) -\operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n) = \overline{t}_{}({u_n})$.
	Hence, $i=0$ and $W$ is minimal.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- observation-6: body_md -->
\label{minimal-tlp-and-rtl-properties}
	Let $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$ be a time discretisation and $W=u_0\ldots u_n$ be a minimal too-long path that is $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long.
	Then $\pi_{W}(i) > \underline{t}_{}({u_i})$ for all $i>0$.
<!-- end observation-6 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
Let $W=u_0\ldots u_n$ be a minimal too-long path that is $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long and $i>0$.
	Since $u_iW$ is not too-long, $\underline{t}_{}({u_i}) + \tau(u_iW) \leq \overline{t}_{}({u_n}) < \pi_{W}(n) \leq \pi_{W}(i) + \tau(u_iW)$, so $\underline{t}_{}({u_i}) < \pi_{W}(i)$.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lemma:relaxedtoolongtimepoints}
	A too-long path $W = u_0b_1u_1\ldots b_nu_n$ is $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long if and only if for every $i \in (n]$ there is a time point $t_i \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{b_i}$ such that
	\begin{enumprop}
		\item $t_1 \leq \underline{t}_{}({u_0})$,\label{lemma:relaxedtoolongtimepoints:start}
		\item $t_i + \tau(b_i) \geq t_{i+1}$ for all $i \in (n)$, \label{lemma:relaxedtoolongtimepoints:linking}
		\item and $t_n + \tau(b_n) > \overline{t}_{}({u_n})$.\label{lemma:relaxedtoolongtimepoints:end}
	\end{enumprop}
	Additionally, $t_i \in I_W(i) = [t_{W}(i-1) - \operatorname{\Delta_L}(u_n,t_{W}(n)) + 1, t_{W}(i-1)]$ for all $i \in (n]$.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Evidently, $\pi_{W}(i) - \tau(b_i)\in \ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{b_i}$ for $i > 0$.
	Assume $W$ is relaxed too-long. Then we can set $t_i = \pi_{W}(i) - \tau(b_i)$.
	Then, by definition of $\pi_{W}(i)$, all three conditions hold.
	
	Conversely, assume that $t_i$ fulfilling the conditions exist. 
	Inductively, $\pi_{W}(i-1) \geq t_i$: the base case follows from~\cref{lemma:relaxedtoolongtimepoints:start}, and since $t_i\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{b_i}$, we obtain $\pi_{W}(i) = \lfloor\pi_{W}(i-1)\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{b_i}} + \tau(b_i) \geq t_i + \tau(b_i)$.
	For $i<n$, this is at least $t_{i+1}$ by~\cref{lemma:relaxedtoolongtimepoints:linking}; for $i=n$, it is greater than $\overline{t}_{}({u_n})$ by~\cref{lemma:relaxedtoolongtimepoints:end}.
	Thus, $W$ is relaxed too-long.
	
	For the additional statement, observe that \eqref{lemma:relaxedtoolongtimepoints:start} and \eqref{lemma:relaxedtoolongtimepoints:linking} together give
	\begin{equation*}
		t_i \leq t_1 + \sum_{j = 1}^{i-1}\tau(b_j) \leq \underline{t}_{}({u_0}) + \sum_{j =1}^{i-1}\tau(b_j) = t_{W}(i-1).
	\end{equation*}
	Similarly, \cref{lemma:relaxedtoolongtimepoints:linking,lemma:relaxedtoolongtimepoints:end} give
	\begin{equation*}
		t_i + \sum_{j=i}^n \tau(b_j) \geq t_n + \tau(b_n) > \overline{t}_{}({u_n})
	\end{equation*}
	and therefore
	\begin{equation*}
		t_i > \overline{t}_{}({u_n}) - \sum_{j=i}^n \tau(b_j)
		= t_{W}(i-1) - \operatorname{\Delta_L}(u_n,t_{W}(n)).
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
\textcite{shu_new_2026} also proposed a mixed-integer program for this problem (using their slightly stronger elimination criterion).
	Due to their differing definition of too-long paths that may start only at origin nodes $(o_k,k)$, the MIP is significantly more complex and not reproduced here.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{theorem:real_arrival_time_eliminates}
	Let $W = b_1\ldots b_n$ denote a too-long path in $D_S$.
	For a discretisation $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$ with $t_{W}(i-1) \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{b_i}$ for $i \in (n]$, $W$ is $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long (and thus eliminated).
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
Consider an instance on the network depicted in~\cref{example:pathological}, which consists of the path $P=v_0\ldots v_n$ together with the nodes $v'$ and $v''$ as well as arcs $v_0v'$, $v'v_1$, $v_{n-1}v''$, and $v''v_{n}$.
	Arcs $v_0v_1$ and $v_{n-1}v_n$ have length~3 and all others have length~1.
	Commodities $k_1$ and $k_2$ must both be transported from $v_0$ to $v_n$.
	Note that the nodes $v'$ and $v''$ exist simply to make $\underline{t}_{k}({v})$ and $\overline{t}_{k}({v})$ irrelevant for nodes $v_1$ to $v_{n-1}$.
	\begin{figure}[tbp]
		\centering
		\begin{tikzpicture}
			\node[draw,label={[align=left] above:{$r_{k_1}= 0$\\$r_{k_2}=1$}}] (v0) {$v_0$}; 
			\node[draw, right=3cm of v0] (v1) {$v_1$}; 
			\node[draw, below right=of v0] (vp) {$v'$};
			\node[draw, right=of v1] (v2) {$v_{2}$};
			\node[draw, right=of v2] (vn) {$v_{n-1}$};
			\node[draw, below right=of vn] (vup) {$v''$};
			\node[draw,label={[align=left]above:{$\ell_{k_1}=n+4$\\$\ell_{k_2}=n+5$}}, right=3cm of vn] (vend) {$v_n$};
			
			\draw[->] (v0) to  node[above] {$3$} (v1);
			\draw[->] (v1) to  node[above] {$1$} (v2);
			\draw[->,white,text=black] (v2) to  node {$\dots$} (vn);
			\draw[->] (vn) to  node[below left] {$1$} (vup);
			\draw[->] (v0) to  node[below left] {$1$} (vp);
			\draw[->] (vn) to  node[above] {$3$} (vend);
			\draw[->] (vup) to node[below right] {$1$} (vend);
			\draw[->] (vp) to node[below right] {$1$} (v1);
\end{tikzpicture}
		\caption{Example of an instance with exponentially many too-long paths.
			Arc weights represent travel time $\tau$.}
		\label{example:pathological}
	\end{figure}
	
	The path $P$ has length $n + 4$, so each commodity could traverse this path by itself and arrive on time, but when consolidated, $k_1$ arrives too late.
	If we consider the dispatch-node graph for the solution that consolidates them on all arcs of $P$, we see that the too-long paths must start at $({v_0},{k_2})$ and end at $({v_n},{k_1})$, since this is the only way to achieve an arrival time of $n+5$ and the only way to reach the only deadline this violates.
	However, the nodes in between are irrelevant, so we can choose either $({v_i},{k_1})$ or $({v_i},{k_2})$ for all intermediate $i\in(n)$.
	Thus, we obtain $2^{n-1}$ minimal too-long paths in this example.


	Despite this abundance of minimal too-long paths, it suffices to add the time points $1$ on arc $v_0v_1$ and $i+2$ on the arcs $v_{i-1}v_{i}$ for $i \in [2,n]$ to eliminate them all (by making them relaxed too-long).
<!-- end example-2 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
The \emph{time-expanded too-long path graph $D_\mathcal{W}$} is given by
	\begin{align*}
		 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{

        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
 &= 
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { (u_i, t_{W}(i)) : W=u_0\ldots u_n\in\mathcal{W}, i\in [n] } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiter(u_i, t_{W}(i)) : W=u_0\ldots u_n\in\mathcal{W}, i\in [n]\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{(u_i, t_{W}(i)) : W=u_0\ldots u_n\in\mathcal{W}, i\in [n]\right\}} 
	 }
 
, \\
		 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{A}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt A}D_\mathcal{W}} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
 &= 
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { (u_i,t_{W}(i))(u_{i+1},t_{W}(i+1)) : W=u_0\ldots u_n\in\mathcal{W}, i\in [n) } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiter(u_i,t_{W}(i))(u_{i+1},t_{W}(i+1)) : W=u_0\ldots u_n\in\mathcal{W}, i\in [n)\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{(u_i,t_{W}(i))(u_{i+1},t_{W}(i+1)) : W=u_0\ldots u_n\in\mathcal{W}, i\in [n)\right\}} 
	 }
 
.
	\end{align*}
	For notational purposes, we identify a node $w=(u,t)$ with its first component $u$, letting us write $\underline{t}_{}({w})$ or $a(w)$.

	We also define $\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$ and $\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$ to be the sets of nodes in $D_\mathcal{W}$ originating from the initial and final nodes of walks in $\mathcal{W}$.
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- notationenum-1: body_md -->
\begin{itemize}

		\item For a path $P=w_0\ldots w_n$ in $D_\mathcal{W}$ with $w_i=(u_i,t_i)$ for $i\in[n]$, we write $W_P$ for the walk $u_0\ldots u_n$ in $D_S$.
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end notationenum-1 -->

<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
By \cref{used-buffer-lateness-minimal-tlp}, the nodes $w\in D_\mathcal{W}$ that have $\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w)=0$ are exactly the initial nodes of minimal too-long paths.
	Similarly, the nodes with $\operatorname{\Delta_L}(w)>0$ are exactly the final nodes of minimal too-long paths.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- corollaryenum-1: body_md -->
\label{tlp-properties-in-tetlp}
	\begin{enumprop}
		\item If $P$ is an $\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$-path in $D_\mathcal{W}$, then $W_P$ is too-long.
		\item If $P$ is a path in $D_\mathcal{W}$ and $W_P$ is too-long, then $P$ ends at a node in $\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$.
	\end{enumprop}
<!-- end corollaryenum-1 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
We notice that each arc $(u,t)(u',t')$ in $D_\mathcal{W}$ satisfies $t' = t + \tau(uu')$, giving us that any path $P$ from $w=(u,t)$ to $w'=(u',t')$ satisfies $t' = t + \tau(W_P)$.
	Now let $P=w_0\ldots w_n$ be a path in $D_\mathcal{W}$ with $w_i=(u_i,t_i)$ for all $i\in[n]$.
	
	For the first part, let $P$ be an $\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$-path. Then $\underline{t}_{}({u_0}) + \tau(W_P) = t_0 + \tau(W_P) = t_n > \overline{t}_{}({u_n})$, by \cref{SW-BW-characterisation}.
	Thus, $W_P$ is too-long.
	
	For the second part, $\overline{t}_{}({w_n}) < \underline{t}_{}({w_0}) + \tau(W_P)$ since this path is too-long.
	Using \cref{SW-BW-characterisation}, $\underline{t}_{}({w_0}) \leq t_0$, giving us $\overline{t}_{}({w_n}) < t_0 + \tau(W_P) = t_n$, so $w_n\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$.
<!-- end proof-11 -->

<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{criterion:tetlpg}
	Let $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$ be a time discretisation.
	Then all minimal too-long paths in $D_S$ are $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long if and only if $\pi_{w}  > \overline{t}_{}({w})$ for all $w\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
We start with the backward implication for which we need the following claim.
	\begin{claim}
		For all $w=(u,t)\in 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{V}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt V}\!D_\mathcal{W}} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
$ and for all $W\in\mathcal{W}$ for which $u$ is the $i$th node and $t=t_{W}(i)$, we get $\pi_{w} \leq \pi_{W}(i)$.
	\end{claim}
	\begin{claimproof}
		We proceed by induction on a topological ordering. For nodes $w=(u,t)\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$, if $u$ occurs in $W\in\mathcal{W}$, then $u$ is the initial node and $\pi_{w} = \underline{t}_{}({u}) =\pi_{W}(0)$.
		
		For the induction step, we let $w=(u,t)\in 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{V}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt V}\!D_\mathcal{W}} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
\setminus\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$, $W\in\mathcal{W}$ contain $u$ as its $i$th node, and $t=t_{W}(i)$.
		Let $u'$ be the predecessor of $u$ on $W$ and $t' = t_{W}(i-1)$.
		Then $w'=(u',t')$ is a predecessor of $w$ in $D_\mathcal{W}$.
		Thus, using the induction hypothesis, we conclude that $\pi_{w} \leq \lfloor\pi_{w'}\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{w'w}} + \tau(w'w) \leq \lfloor\pi_{W}(i-1)\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{u'u}} + \tau(u'u) = \pi_{W}(i)$.
	\end{claimproof}
	
	Now let $W=u_0\ldots u_n\in\mathcal{W}$.
	We know that $w=(u_n,t_{W}(n))\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$ and $\overline{t}_{}({w}) < \pi_{w} \leq \pi_{W}(n)$, so $W$ is $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long.
	
	For the forward implication, we need to associate the nodes of $D_\mathcal{W}$ with walks in $D_S$ that realise their relaxed arrival times.
	\begin{claim}
		\label{path-realising-ratw}
		For each $w\in 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{V}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt V}\!D_\mathcal{W}} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
$ there exists a path $P=w_0\ldots w_n$ in $D_\mathcal{W}$ such that $w_n = w$, $w_0\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$, and, for all $i\in[n]$,
		\begin{enumprop}
			\item $t_{W_P}(i) = t_i$ and 
			\item $\pi_{W_P}(i) = \pi_{w_i}$.
		\end{enumprop}
	\end{claim}
	\begin{claimproof}
		We prove the claim by induction on some topological ordering, starting with $w_0=(u_0,t_0)\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$ once more.
		In this case, we can take the trivial path $P=w_0$ and get $t_{W_P}(0) = \underline{t}_{}({u_0}) = t_0$ and $\pi_{w_0} = \underline{t}_{}({u_0}) = \pi_{W_P}(0)$.
		
		For the induction step, let $w=(u,t)\in 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{V}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt V}\!D_\mathcal{W}} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
\setminus\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$.
		Then $\pi_{w} = \lfloor\pi_{w'}\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{w'w}} + \tau(w'w)$ for some $w'=(u',t')\in\ifthenelse{\isempty{}}{ 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{w}}{ 
		\ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{w}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt N^{\text{in}}}w} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}\parentheses{w}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}\parentheses{w}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
}{ 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{w}}{ 
		\ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}_{}}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{w}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt N^{\text{in}}_{}}w} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}_{}\parentheses{w}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{N^{\text{in}}_{}\parentheses{w}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
}$.
		By definition of the arcs, $t' = t - \tau(w'w)$.
		
		Then, by induction, we get a path $P'=w_0\ldots w_{n'}$ in $D_\mathcal{W}$ with $w_0\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$, $w_{n'}=w'$, and, for all $i\in[n']$, $w_i=(u_i,t_i)$, $t_{W_{P'}}(i) = t_i$ and $\pi_{w_i} = \pi_{W_{P'}}(i)$.
		Let $P=w_0\ldots w_{n'}w$ and $n=n'+1$. Then $w_0\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$, $w_n=w$ and the remaining two properties continue to hold for $i\in[n']$.
		For $n$ we get $t_{W_P}(n) = t_{W_P}(n') + \tau(w'w) = t = t_n$ and $\pi_{w} = \lfloor\pi_{w'}\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{w'w}} + \tau(w'w) = \lfloor\pi_{W_P}(n')\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{u'u}} + \tau(u'u) = \pi_{W_P}(n)$, as desired.
	\end{claimproof}
	
	We can also show a lower bound on the values $\pi_{w}$.
	\begin{claim}
		\label{ratw-at-least-edt}
		For all $w\in  
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{V}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt V}\!D_\mathcal{W}} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
$, $\pi_{w} \geq \underline{t}_{}({w})$.
	\end{claim}
	\begin{claimproof}
		We prove the result inductively, where it clearly holds for nodes in $\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$.
		So, let $w=(u,t)\in 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{V}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt V}\!D_\mathcal{W}} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
\setminus\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$.
		By definition of $D_\mathcal{W}$, $w$ is on $P=P_W$ for some minimal too-long path $W\in\mathcal{W}$.
		By \cref{path-realising-ratw} we obtain a $w_0w$-path $P'$ in $D_\mathcal{W}$ and corresponding walk $W'=W_{P'}$ with $w_0\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$ that realises the relaxed arrival times.
		
		We now regard the splicings $P'' = P'wP = w_0\ldots w_n$ and $W'' = W'uW = u_0\ldots u_n$.
		Since $P''$ starts in $\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$ and ends in $\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$, $W''$ is too-long by \cref{tlp-properties-in-tetlp}.
		It thus contains a minimal too-long path $W^*=u_iW''u_j$, meaning $P^*=w_iP''w_j$ ends in $\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$ by \cref{tlp-properties-in-tetlp}.
		Hence, $j=n$.
		
		Since $W$ is a minimal too-long path and $w\notin\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$, $uW$ is not too-long, and, thus, $u=u_s$ is in $W^*$.
		Consequently, $i<s$, $u_i\in W'$, and $\pi_{w_i} = \pi_{W'}(i)$.
		By induction, we know that $\pi_{W'}(i) = \pi_{w_i}\geq \underline{t}_{}({w_i}) = \pi_{W^*}(0)$.
		Therefore, $\pi_{W'}(i+\ell) \geq \pi_{W^*}(\ell)$ for all $\ell\leq s-i$, in particular, $\pi_{w} = \pi_{W'}(s) \geq \pi_{W^*}(s-i)$ holds at $w$.
		By \cref{minimal-tlp-and-rtl-properties}, $\pi_{W^*}(s-i) > \underline{t}_{}({w})$, so $\pi_{w} > \underline{t}_{}({w})$ and the claim follows.
	\end{claimproof}
	
	Now let $w\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$.
	\Cref{path-realising-ratw} yields a path $P=w_0\ldots w_n$ in $D_\mathcal{W}$ with $w_i=(u_i,t_i)$ for all $i\in[n]$ and $w_0\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$ and $w_n=w$.
	Again, we obtain that $W_P$ is a too-long path and contains a minimal too-long path $W^* = u_iW_P u_n$, by \cref{tlp-properties-in-tetlp}.

	Since $W^*$ is $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long, $\pi_{W^*}(n-i) > \overline{t}_{}({u_n}) = \overline{t}_{}({w})$.
	Moreover, by \cref{ratw-at-least-edt}, $\pi_{W_P}(i) = \pi_{w_i} \geq \underline{t}_{}({w_i}) = \pi_{W^*}(0)$ and, inductively, $\pi_{W_P}(i+\ell) \geq \pi_{W^*}(\ell)$.
	Thus, $\pi_{w} = \pi_{W_P}(n) \geq \pi_{W^*}(n-i) > \overline{t}_{}({w})$ as desired.
<!-- end proof-12 -->

        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
$, $\pi_{w} \geq \underline{t}_{}({w})$.
<!-- end claim-3 -->
<!-- claimproof-3: body_md -->
We prove the result inductively, where it clearly holds for nodes in $\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$.
		So, let $w=(u,t)\in 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{V}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt V}\!D_\mathcal{W}} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
\setminus\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$.
		By definition of $D_\mathcal{W}$, $w$ is on $P=P_W$ for some minimal too-long path $W\in\mathcal{W}$.
		By \cref{path-realising-ratw} we obtain a $w_0w$-path $P'$ in $D_\mathcal{W}$ and corresponding walk $W'=W_{P'}$ with $w_0\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$ that realises the relaxed arrival times.
		
		We now regard the splicings $P'' = P'wP = w_0\ldots w_n$ and $W'' = W'uW = u_0\ldots u_n$.
		Since $P''$ starts in $\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$ and ends in $\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$, $W''$ is too-long by \cref{tlp-properties-in-tetlp}.
		It thus contains a minimal too-long path $W^*=u_iW''u_j$, meaning $P^*=w_iP''w_j$ ends in $\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$ by \cref{tlp-properties-in-tetlp}.
		Hence, $j=n$.
		
		Since $W$ is a minimal too-long path and $w\notin\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$, $uW$ is not too-long, and, thus, $u=u_s$ is in $W^*$.
		Consequently, $i<s$, $u_i\in W'$, and $\pi_{w_i} = \pi_{W'}(i)$.
		By induction, we know that $\pi_{W'}(i) = \pi_{w_i}\geq \underline{t}_{}({w_i}) = \pi_{W^*}(0)$.
		Therefore, $\pi_{W'}(i+\ell) \geq \pi_{W^*}(\ell)$ for all $\ell\leq s-i$, in particular, $\pi_{w} = \pi_{W'}(s) \geq \pi_{W^*}(s-i)$ holds at $w$.
		By \cref{minimal-tlp-and-rtl-properties}, $\pi_{W^*}(s-i) > \underline{t}_{}({w})$, so $\pi_{w} > \underline{t}_{}({w})$ and the claim follows.
<!-- end claimproof-3 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
We note that we only needed to use the set $\mathcal{W}$ of all minimal too-long paths in the proof above to get the forward implication.
	For the converse, any set $\mathcal{W}'$ of minimal too-long paths would have worked, so, indeed, if $\pi_{w} > \overline{t}_{}({w})$ for all $w\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_{\mathcal{W}'}$, then all minimal too-long paths in $\mathcal{W}'$ are $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{candgraph-contains-exgraph}
	\Cref{algo:candidate} runs in $\mathcal{O}(\ensuremath{\lvert 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_S}}{ 
		\ensuremath{A}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_S}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt A}D_S} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_S}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_S}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
\rvert}\cdot H)$ time and the computed \emph{candidate graph} $D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow$ satisfies $D_\mathcal{W} \subseteq D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow$.
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
For the time complexity, note that $L$ can only contain nodes $(u,t)$ with $0\leq \underline{t}_{}({u}) \leq t \leq \overline{t}_{}({u}) < H$.
	Since we process the nodes with increasing $t$, no node is processed twice and the iterations of the inner for loop are thus bounded by $\ensuremath{\lvert 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_S}}{ 
		\ensuremath{A}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_S}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt A}D_S} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_S}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_S}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
\rvert}\cdot H$.
	Extracting the node $(u,t)$ from $L$ can be done in constant time by using a separate list for each of the $H$ lengths in question.
	All other operations are easy to do in constant time, yielding the desired running time.
	
	To see that $D_\mathcal{W}\subseteqD_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow$, let $W=u_0\ldots u_n\in\mathcal{W}$ and let $P_W = w_0\ldots w_n$.
	We show by induction that $P_W\subseteq D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow$, which also shows $D_\mathcal{W}\subseteqD_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow$ since $W\in\mathcal{W}$ was arbitrary.
	
	By~\cref{algo:candidate:basecopies}, $w_0 \in D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow$.
	Now let $w_i\in D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow$ for $i<n$. Then $\operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_i)\leq 0$ by \cref{used-buffer-lateness-minimal-tlp}.
	Thus, $w_i$ was added to $L$ in some iteration of \cref{algo:candidate} and, when it is removed, $u_{i+1} \in \ifthenelse{\isempty{}}{ 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{u_i}}{ 
		\ensuremath{N^{\text{out}}}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{u_i}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt N^{\text{out}}}u_i} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{N^{\text{out}}\parentheses{u_i}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{N^{\text{out}}\parentheses{u_i}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
}{ 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{u_i}}{ 
		\ensuremath{N^{\text{out}}_{}}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{u_i}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt N^{\text{out}}_{}}u_i} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{N^{\text{out}}_{}\parentheses{u_i}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{N^{\text{out}}_{}\parentheses{u_i}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
}$.
	Since $\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w_{i+1}) > 0$ by \cref{used-buffer-lateness-minimal-tlp}, the next node $w_{i+1}$ as well as the arc $w_iw_{i+1}$ are in $D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow$, too.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{arcs-in-exgraph}
	An arc $ww'\in D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow$ is in $D_\mathcal{W}$ if and only if there exists a $\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace'\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace'$-path $P=w_0\ldots w_n$ in $D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow$ that contains $ww'$ and for which $\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w_i) \geq \operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n)$ holds for all $i\in(n]$.
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
The forward implication follows from the preceding argument.
	For the converse, let $P=w_0\ldots w_n$ be a path in $D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow$ that contains $ww'$ and for which $\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w_i) \geq \operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n)$ holds for all $i\in(n]$.
	Then $W_P$ is a walk in $D_S$ for which the properties of \cref{used-buffer-lateness-minimal-tlp-equiv} are satisfied:
	the first is guaranteed by our construction already since we do not add outgoing arcs to nodes with positive lateness and $\operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n)>0$ holds since $w_n\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace'$.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{marking-rule} 
	An arc $ww' \in  
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow}}{ 
		\ensuremath{A}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt A}D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
$ is in $D_\mathcal{W}$ if and only if
	\begin{equation}\label{marking-rule:eq}
		\operatorname{req}(w') < \infty \qquad \text{and} \qquad \operatorname{cap}(w) \geq \operatorname{req}(w').
	\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
Similarly, if $ww'\in  
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow}}{ 
		\ensuremath{A}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt A}D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
$ is in $D_\mathcal{W}$, then \cref{arcs-in-exgraph} yields a path $P=w_0\ldots w_n$ in $D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow$ that contains $ww'$ and for which $\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w_i) \geq \operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n)$ holds for all $i\in(n]$.
	In particular, $w$ is $\operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n)$-reachable and $w'$ is $\operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n)$-extendable, yielding $\operatorname{cap}(w) \geq \operatorname{req}(w')$ and $\operatorname{req}(w') < \infty$.
	
	Conversely, assume \cref{marking-rule:eq} holds for the arc $ww'$.
	Let $P_\ell=w_0\ldots w_k$, with $w_k=w$, be a path realising $\operatorname{cap}(w)$ and $P_r=w_{k+1}\ldots w_n$, with $w_{k+1}=w'$, be a path realising $\operatorname{req}(w')$, which exists since the value is finite by assumption.
	Then $\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w_i) \geq \operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n) = \operatorname{req}(w')$ for all $i\geq k+1$ and $\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w_i) \geq \operatorname{cap}(w) \geq \operatorname{req}(w') = \operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n)$ for $0<i\leq k$.
	Hence, the path $P_{\ell}P_r$ is a $\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace'\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace'$-path that contains $ww'$ and satisfies the condition of \cref{arcs-in-exgraph}, so $ww'\inD_\mathcal{W}$.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- example-3: body_md -->
Consider the dispatch-node graph $D_S$ in~\cref{fig:markingrule:dispatchgraph} and the corresponding candidate graph $D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow$ in~\cref{fig:markingrule:candidategraph}.
	The minimal too-long paths are $u_1u_2$ and $u_0u_1u_3$:
	the path $u_1u_3$ arrives at time $4$ and is feasible, whereas $u_0u_1u_3$ arrives at time $5 > \overline{t}_{}({u_3})$.
	The check of \cref{marking-rule} keeps their time-expanded arcs and rejects the arc from $(u_1,2)$ to $(u_2,5)$.
	This also lets us remove the now isolated nodes $(u_2,5)$, $(u_3,2)$, and $(u_2,2)$.

				\node[draw, right=of u1,label={[align=left]right:{$[2,3]$}}] (u2) {$u_2$};
				\node[draw, below=0.65cm of u2,
				label={[align=left]below:{$[2,4]$}}] (u3) {$u_3$};
				
				\draw[->] (u0) to node[below] {$1$} (u1);
				\draw[->] (u1) to node[below] {$3$} (u2);
				\draw[->] (u1) to node[below left] {$3$} (u3);
				
			\end{tikzpicture}
			\caption{A dispatch-node graph $D_S$.}
			\label{fig:markingrule:dispatchgraph}
		\end{subfigure}\hfill

\draw[->] (u0t1) to node[above] {$\checkmark$} (u1t2);
				\draw[->] (u1t1) to node[above] {$\checkmark$} (u2t4);
				\draw[->] (u1t2) to node[above] {$\times$} (u2t5);
				\draw[->] (u1t2) to node[above] {$\checkmark$} (u3t5);
			\end{tikzpicture}
			\caption{The corresponding candidate graph $D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow$.}

			The nodes $u$ in the dispatch-node graph are labelled with $[\underline{t}_{}({u}),\overline{t}_{}({u})]$.

<!-- end example-3 -->

<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
We start with the recurrence for $\operatorname{cap}(w)$ and inductively obtain that the formula is correct, where this is true for all nodes in $\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace'$.
	For the remaining nodes, $\operatorname{cap}(w)$ is determined by a path to $w$ whose nodes have a minimum slack consumption that is as large as possible.
	Thus, it uses a predecessor $w'$ of maximum $\operatorname{cap}(w')$, without loss of generality, and $\operatorname{cap}(w) = \min
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { \operatorname{cap}(w'),\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w) } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiter\operatorname{cap}(w'),\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w)\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{\operatorname{cap}(w'),\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w)\right\}} 
	 }
 
$.
	
	For $\operatorname{req}(w)$, we again see that the value for $w\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace'$ is set correctly since $\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w)\geq \operatorname{\Delta_L}(w)$.
	For the remaining nodes, the path determining $\operatorname{req}(w)$ (if it exists) uses a successor $w'$ that minimises $\operatorname{req}(w')$ subject to $\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w)\geq \operatorname{req}(w')$, and its requirement is then identical.
	If $\operatorname{req}(w)=\infty$, then any path to a node in $\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace'$ has insufficient slack consumption; in particular, $\operatorname{\Delta_S}(w) < \operatorname{req}(w')$ for all successors $w'$.

	Since for either recurrence each node and arc is processed exactly once, the procedure completes in $\mathcal{O}(\ensuremath{\lvert 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}^\uparrow}}{ 
		\ensuremath{V}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}

            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt V}\!D_\tlpsymb^\uparrow} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\tlpsymb^\uparrow}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\tlpsymb^\uparrow}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
\rvert} + \ensuremath{\lvert

<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{characterisation-IP-calW-rtl}
	All minimal too-long paths are $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long if and only if there exist time points $t(w'w)\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{w'w}$ for all $w'w\in

$ and $\pi(w)$ for all $w\in

	} 
$ such that
	\begin{enumprop}
		\item $\pi(w) \leq \underline{t}_{}({w})$ for all $w\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$,
		\item $t(w'w) \leq \pi(w')$ for all $w'w\in

	} 
$,\label{prop:calW-rtl-2}
		\item $t(w'w) + \tau(w'w) \geq \pi(w)$ for all $w'w\in

$, and

	Additionally, for $w'w\in

<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Note that all minimal too-long paths are $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long if and only if the relaxed arrival times $\pi_{w}$ at the boundary nodes $w\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$ satisfy $\pi_{w} > \overline{t}_{}({w})$, by \cref{criterion:tetlpg}.
	
	For the forward implication, we use $\pi(w) = \pi_{w}$ and $t(w'w) = \lfloor\pi_{w'}\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{w'w}}$.
	Then, for $w\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$, $\pi(w) = \pi_{w} = \underline{t}_{}({w})$ and, for $w\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$, $\pi(w) = \pi_{w} > \overline{t}_{}({w})$.
	For $w'w\in 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{A}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt A}D_\mathcal{W}} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
$, $t(w'w) = \lfloor\pi_{w'}\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{w'w}} \leq \pi_{w'} = \pi(w')$ and $t(w'w) + \tau(w'w) = \lfloor\pi_{w'}\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{w'w}} + \tau(w'w) \geq \pi_{w} = \pi(w)$.
	
	For the converse, let $t$ and $\pi$ satisfy the four specified properties.
	Then, inductively, $\pi(w) \leq \pi_{w}$ for all $w\in 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{V}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt V}\!D_\mathcal{W}} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
$, which implies that $\pi_{w} \geq \pi(w) > \overline{t}_{}({w})$ for all $w\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{B}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$.
	For $w\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{S}}\xspace_\mathcal{W}$ this is clear and for the other nodes $w$ we note that \cref{prop:calW-rtl-2} implies that $t(w'w) \leq \lfloor\pi(w')\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{w'w}}$ since $t(w'w)\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{w'w}$.
	Using this, we obtain $\pi(w) \leq t(w'w) + \tau(w'w) \leq \lfloor\pi(w')\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{w'w}} + \tau(w'w) \leq \lfloor\pi_{w'}\rfloor_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{w'w}} + \tau(w'w)$.
	Since this holds for all predecessors $w'$ of $w$, $\pi(w) \leq \pi_{w}$.
	
	For the final part, let $w'w\in 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{A}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt A}D_\mathcal{W}} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
$ with $w'=(u',t')$.
	Choose $W\in\mathcal{W}$ of minimum lateness $\ell$ among those whose time-expanded path $P_W=w_0\ldots w_n$ contains $w'w$.
	Inductively, $t(w'w) \leq \pi(w') \leq \pi(w_0) + \tau(P_W w') \leq \underline{t}_{}({w_0}) + \tau(P_W w') = t'$.
	
	For the other direction, $t(w'w) \geq \pi(w_n) - \tau(w'P_W) > \overline{t}_{}({w_n}) - \tau(w'P_W) = t' - \operatorname{\Delta_L}(w_n) = t'-\ell$.
	By \cref{used-buffer-lateness-minimal-tlp}, the suffix $wP_W$ shows that $w$ is $\ell$-extendable.
	It cannot be $\ell'$-extendable for some $\ell'<\ell$ since, otherwise, we can extend the prefix $P_W w$ by that suffix, preserving $w'w$, to obtain a minimal too-long path of lower lateness by \cref{used-buffer-lateness-minimal-tlp-equiv}.
	Thus, $\operatorname{req}(w)=\ell$ and $t(w'w)\in(t'-\operatorname{req}(w),t']$.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- definition-11: body_md -->
Given a solution $\Phi$, its \emph{partition-linking graph} $D_\Phi$ is  given by
	\begin{align*}
		 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\Phi}}{

	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}

        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\Phi}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\Phi}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
 &= \Phi \\
		 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\Phi}}{ 
		\ensuremath{A}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\Phi}

	{
