            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt A}D_\Phi} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\Phi}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\Phi}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
 &= 
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { \phi(w_1w_2)\phi(w_2w_3) \mid w_1w_2,w_2w_3 \in  
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{A}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt A}D_\tlpsymb} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\tlpsymb}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\tlpsymb}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
  } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiter\phi(w_1w_2)\phi(w_2w_3) \mid w_1w_2,w_2w_3 \in  
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{A}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\tlpsymb}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt \arcset}\exgraph} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\arcset\parentheses{\exgraph}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{\arcset\parentheses{\exgraph}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
 \relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{\phi(w_1w_2)\phi(w_2w_3) \mid w_1w_2,w_2w_3 \in  
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{A}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt A}D_\tlpsymb} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\tlpsymb}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_\tlpsymb}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
 \right\}} 
	 }
 
.
	\end{align*}
	For arcs $\phi'\phi$ we set $\tau(\phi'\phi) = \tau(a(\phi'))$ and for nodes $\phi$ we set
	\[
		\underline{t}_{}({\phi}) = \max 
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { \min I_{w'w} : w'w \in \phi } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiter\min I_{w'w} : w'w \in \phi\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{\min I_{w'w} : w'w \in \phi\right\}} 
	 }
 
,
		\qquad
		\overline{t}_{}({\phi}) = \min
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { \max I_{w'w} : w'w \in \phi } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiter\max I_{w'w} : w'w \in \phi\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{\max I_{w'w} : w'w \in \phi\right\}} 
	 }
 
.
	\]
<!-- end definition-11 -->
<!-- definition-12: body_md -->
A $\phi'\phi$-path $P$ in $D_\Phi$ is \emph{too-short} if $\overline{t}_{}({\phi'}) + \tau(P) < \underline{t}_{}({\phi})$.
<!-- end definition-12 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
A solution $\Phi$ is feasible for the ORP if and only if $D_\Phi$ contains no too-short path.
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
Let $\Phi$ be feasible with time points $t$ and $\pi$.
	Moreover, let $P=\phi_1\ldots\phi_n$ be a path in $D_\Phi$.
	For any arc $\phi_i\phi_{i+1}$ we get nodes $w_1,\, w_2,\, w_3\in 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{V}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt V}\!D_\mathcal{W}} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
$ such that $\phi_i=\phi(w_1w_2)$ and $\phi_{i+1}=\phi(w_2w_3)$.

<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lemma:orp_ddd_time_points}
	Let $\Phi$ be a solution to \fontfamily{cmr}\textup{\textsc{ORP}}\xspace, $P = \phi_1\ldots\phi_n$ be a too-short path in $D_\Phi$, and $t_i = \overline{t}_{}({\phi_1}) + \tau(P\phi_i)$.
	If $\ensuremath{\mathcal{D}}\xspace$ is a discretisation with $t_i + 1 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{D}}\xspace_{a(\phi_i)}$ for $i \in (n]$, then $\Phi$ is not feasible for \fontfamily{cmr}\textup{\textsc{R-ORP}}\xspace.
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Suppose $\Phi$ was feasible for \fontfamily{cmr}\textup{\textsc{R-ORP}}\xspace, then we obtain intervals $h(\varphi)\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{D}}\xspace_{a(\phi)}^I$ that satisfy all five conditions in the definition of the relaxation.
	For any arc $\phi_i\phi_{i+1}$ we get nodes $w_1,\, w_2,\, w_3\in 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_\mathcal{W}}}{ 
		\ensuremath{V}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_\mathcal{W}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt V}\!D_\mathcal{W}} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{V\parentheses{D_\mathcal{W}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
$ such that $\phi_i=\phi(w_1w_2)$ and $\phi_{i+1}=\phi(w_2w_3)$.
	By feasibility, $\max h(\phi_i) = \max h(\phi(w_1w_2)) \geq \pi(w_2) - \tau(w_1w_2) \geq \min h(\phi(w_2w_3)) - \tau(a(\phi_i)) = \min h(\phi_{i+1}) - \tau(\phi_i\phi_{i+1})$.
	
	Note that $\min h(\phi_1) \leq \overline{t}_{}({\phi_1}) = t_1$ and $t_1+1\in\ensuremath{\mathcal{D}}\xspace_{a(\phi_1)}$, so $\max h(\phi_1) \leq t_1$ as well.
	Now, inductively, $\min h(\phi_{i+1}) \leq \max h(\phi_i) + \tau(\phi_i\phi_{i+1}) \leq \overline{t}_{}({\phi_1}) + \tau(P \phi_{i+1}) = t_{i+1}$.
	Again, since $t_{i+1}+1$ is in the discretisation of $a(\phi_{i+1})$, we get $\max h(\phi_{i+1}) \leq t_{i+1}$.
	In particular, $\max h(\phi_n) \leq t_n < \underline{t}_{}({\phi_n}) \leq \max h(\phi_n)$, which is a contradiction.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- definition-13: body_md -->
Let $uu'$ be an arc in $D_S$ with $u=({v},{k})$, and $u'=({v'},{k'})$.
	We define
	\[
		\underline{t}_{}({uu'}) = \max
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { \underline{t}_{k}({v}),\underline{t}_{k'}({v}) } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiter\underline{t}_{k}({v}),\underline{t}_{k'}({v})\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{\underline{t}_{k}({v}),\underline{t}_{k'}({v})\right\}} 
	 }
 
,
		\qquad
		\overline{t}_{}({uu'})=\min
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { \overline{t}_{k}({v'}),\overline{t}_{k'}({v'}) } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiter\overline{t}_{k}({v'}),\overline{t}_{k'}({v'})\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{\overline{t}_{k}({v'}),\overline{t}_{k'}({v'})\right\}} 
	 }
 
.
	\]
	
	We also say that a walk $W=u_0\ldots u_n$ in $D_S$ is a \emph{too-long$^*$ path} if $\underline{t}_{}({u_0u_1}) + \tau(W) > \overline{t}_{}({u_{n-1}u_n})$.
	
	Similarly, we change the initialisation of the relaxed arrival times and set $\pi'_{W}(0) = \underline{t}_{}({u_0u_1})$ and say $W$ is $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long$^*$ if $\pi'_{W}(n) > \overline{t}_{}({u_{n-1}u_n})$.
<!-- end definition-13 -->
<!-- example-4: body_md -->
Consider the partial solution in~\cref{example:sharedarc:routes-2}.
	Assume other paths exist between the nodes such that the unspecified values of $\underline{t}_{k}({v}),\overline{t}_{k}({v})$ are irrelevant.
    There exist two (minimal) too-long paths: $(v_1,k_1)(v_2,k_1)(v_3,k_1)$ and $(v_1,k_1)(v_2,k_1)(v_3,k_2)$. 
    Assume the first arc is fully discretised in $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$; then under our definition, we require one time point in $[2,6]$ on the second arc to make the first path $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long and one time point in $[4,6]$ to make the second path $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long.
    In contrast, we require only a time point in $[2,6]$ to make both paths $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-relaxed too-long$^*$.
    Evidently, this is less restrictive and, depending on the other too-long paths, a smaller discretisation suffices under this definition.
	
	\begin{figure}[htbp]
		\centering
		\begin{tikzpicture}[x=3cm,y=1.2cm]
			\node[draw,label={[align=left]above:{$\underline{t}_{k_1}({v_1})=1$}}] (v1) at (0,0) {$v_1$};
			\node[draw] (v2) at (1,0) {$v_2$};
			\node[draw,label={[align=left]right:{$\overline{t}_{k_1}({v_3})=6$\\$\overline{t}_{k_2}({v_3}) = 8 $}}] (v3) at (2,0) {$v_3$};
            \draw[->] (v1) to node[above] {$
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { k_1 } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiterk_1\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{k_1\right\}} 
	 }
 
$} node[below] {$5$}(v2);
			\draw[->] (v2) to node[above] {$
	
	\oleks_instring:nnnn { : } { k_1,k_2 } {                            
		\ensuremath{\left\{\replacecolonbydelimiterk_1,k_2\relax\right\}} 
	 } {                             
		\ensuremath{\left\{k_1,k_2\right\}} 
	 }
 
$} node[below] {$5$} (v3);
		\end{tikzpicture}
		\caption{Example (partial) solution to demonstrate that fewer time points might suffice.}
		\label{example:sharedarc:routes-2}
	\end{figure}
<!-- end example-4 -->

    } 
 
     
	} 
$ and two different commodities $k_1,k_2 \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}\xspace_{uv}$ with $\underline{t}_{k_2}({u}) + \tau(uv) > \overline{t}_{k_1}({v})$, if there exists a time point $t \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace_{uv}$ with $t \in (\overline{t}_{k_1}({v})- \tau(uv), \underline{t}_{k_2}({u})]$, then $k_1$ and $k_2$ cannot be consolidated in any $\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace$-representable solution.
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
Our definitions of SND and R-SND require each commodity to follow a path (i.e., with no node repetitions).
   For the relaxation and the theoretical results, we could in fact also allow walks; the results remain valid, but the notation becomes cumbersome.
   We could add a constraint 
   \begin{equation*}
    \sum_{(a,I) \in  
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{D_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace,k}}}{

	\l_argument_is_short
	{D_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace,k}}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt A}D_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace,k}} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace,k}}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{A\parentheses{D_{\ensuremath{\mathcal{T}}\xspace,k}}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
 \colon a \in \ifthenelse{\isempty{}}{ 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{v}}{ 
		\ensuremath{A^{\text{out}}}\xspace 
	}{

    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF

        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{ 
             
            \ensuremath{\mathop{\kern0pt A^{\text{out}}}v} 
         
        }{ 
             
            \ensuremath{A^{\text{out}}\parentheses{v}} 
         
        } 
    }{                         
         
            \ensuremath{A^{\text{out}}\parentheses{v}} 
         
    } 
 
     
	} 
}{ 
	\ifthenelse{\isempty{v}}{ 
		\ensuremath{A^{\text{out}}_{}}\xspace 
	}{ 
		 
        \setlength{\shortinputwidth}{\f@size pt+ 1pt}                                          
         
    \regex_extract_once:NnNTF 
	\l_argument_is_short
	{v}             
    \l_argument_seq                                   
	{                     
        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{

}} x_{k,(a,I)}  \leq 1 \quad \forall k \in \ensuremath{\mathcal{K}}\xspace, v \in

	{D}

        \settowidth{\inputwidth}{\ensuremath{\seq_item:Nn \l_argument_seq {2}}} 
        \ifthenelse{\lengthtest{\inputwidth<\shortinputwidth}}{

   \end{equation*} 
   to only obtain paths in the relaxation, but as previously noted~\cite{helber_arc-based_2026}, this tends to make the relaxation harder to solve without substantially improving the lower bound.
   Therefore, we omit this constraint when solving R-SND.
